Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I hope I'm explaining this right, but... I guess what brings me in is that I just can't seem to keep my mind on one thing anymore. It's probably just a minor thing, but at work—I do inventory and freight at a warehouse—every little noise is suddenly distracting me. 

If a beeper goes off, or I hear a forklift alarm, or even just someone moving a pallet nearby, it completely pulls me off my count. And then... well, it's a bit embarrassing to admit, but I'll realize I've accidentally put the wrong numbers into the inventory system. I really pride myself on doing good work and being reliable, so messing up the count has been bothering me. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job, and I was hoping maybe you could help me focus a bit better.

Well... I wouldn't say I'm restless in a bad way. I mean, I know some people get all fidgety and can't sit through a movie, but it's not like that. It's more like... I just have a lot of productive energy lately?

I hope that makes sense... I just want to make sure I'm not making it sound like I'm out of control or anything, because I really do rest just fine when it's time to. Does that answer your question okay?

I hope it doesn't sound like I'm overly anxious or anything, I just really enjoy getting things done.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me a bit more about this feeling of buzzing or being unable to sit still? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well, no, I really don't think so. I mean, I try very hard to just be steady, you know? I don't think I've ever felt so... out of sorts or overly excited that I wasn't still just being me.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I guess if I'm thinking about it, there are some days where I feel... maybe a little more upbeat than usual? Like, I'll wake up feeling really good, almost like I have too much energy, and I'll think, "I can fix the whole house and reorganize the entire warehouse before dinner." And it feels wonderful, honestly, just being able to tackle everything. 

But, um... sometimes, when I'm in that kind of mood, if my husband asks a question about one of my plans or tries to slow me down, I... I might snap at him a little bit. Just over something small, you know? I always feel terrible about it right after. It's not that I'm angry, I just get so focused and moving so fast. I try really hard not to be irritable with him. Is it normal to get a little snappy when you're just trying to get a lot done? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture... I'm really not an angry person, I promise.

Um, well... I do have days where I just have so much energy, even after work. Usually, you know, moving freight and stocking all day, a person would be completely worn out by the time they clock out. But sometimes... I don't know, I just come home buzzing.

It's actually really helpful to get all those chores done, you know? I hope that doesn't sound like I'm doing something wrong. It's just nice to have the energy to keep the house looking nice after a long shift. That's a good thing, isn't it?

I just have a lot of productive energy lately? \n\nLike, usually after a long shift at the warehouse, I'd be pretty worn out. But recently, I come home and I'm just sort of... buzzing, I guess? I just like to keep busy and make sure the house is in top shape. That's a good thing, right? Being productive instead of just being lazy after work? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm overly anxious or anything, I just really enjoy getting things done.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me a bit more about this feeling of buzzing or being unable to sit still? I might snap at him a little bit. Just over something small, you know? I always feel terrible about it right after. It's not that I'm angry, I just get so focused and moving so fast. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I guess if you mean taking on extra things, then yes, sometimes I do that. Like at work, I've started volunteering for these extra safety-improvement projects. And outside of work, I've been signing up for a lot more art workshops than I usually would. 

I just get so motivated, you know? I'll sit down and make all these lists and schedules, almost like I'm running a whole operation or something. It feels really productive to be involved and to learn new things. I mean, it's good to be proactive at your job and have hobbies, right? I just want to make sure I'm doing things that are worthwhile. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm overdoing it... I just like to stay busy and be helpful. Is that the kind of thing you meant?

I don't think my mood has ever been so high that it was a problem or lasted for days and days where I was completely out of control. I'm usually pretty grounded, I think. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, I know I just mentioned getting a little snappy with my husband, and having a lot of energy to do things around the house or at the warehouse, but... I don't really think I've had a time where it lasted for several days in a row and was, like, a completely different mood. 

I really try to keep things pretty steady, you know? I might have a really productive afternoon or a day where I'm just very motivated, but I don't think it's ever been a distinct period of days where I was completely unlike myself. I always try to make sure I'm doing a good job and being a good wife, and I feel like if I was acting really strange for days on end, I would notice, or my husband would say something more serious. I hope I'm answering this right... I just don't think it's ever been quite as extreme or long-lasting as what you're describing. Does that make sense?

Just trying to make sure everyone stays safe. And, well, outside of work, I've been signing up for a few more art workshops than I normally would. I've been making all these lists and schedules to keep it all organized... honestly, it feels a bit like I'm running a whole operation! But I'm managing it all just fine, really. I just like to be involved and make the most of my time. That's a positive thing, isn't it? Being proactive like that?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been unusually high, elated, or irritable lately, where you feel like you don't need as much rest or sleep because you're so energized?\n\nPatient: Well, um... like I said, I really do make sure I get my sleep. But as for my mood... I guess it has been really, really good? Maybe... well, maybe almost a little too upbeat on some days, if that makes sense? I just get this wonderful feeling like I can tackle anything—like I could fix up the whole house and sort out the entire warehouse all before dinner. It's a nice feeling to have that much drive. \n\nBut, um... I do feel a little guilty because... well, if my husband questions one of my plans while I'm trying to do all this, I've noticed I kind of... snap at him? Just over really small things. I don't mean to be irritable at all, I just get so focused on getting everything done for us. It's normal to get a little frustrated when someone interrupts your flow when you're working hard, right? I hope that doesn't make me sound awful, I really do try to be a patient wife.\n\nDoctor: Have there been specific periods of time when you felt unusually energetic, upbeat, or irritable for most of the day, nearly every day, even if only lasting a few days?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no, I don't think so. I mean, I have those days where I'm just feeling really good and getting a lot done, like I mentioned with the art classes and the safety projects. But it's not like it's taking over my whole life for days on end or anything. \n\nI really just have normal good days, you know? Like anyone else. I don't think I've had any stretches where I'm just constantly, unusually... well, whatever you called it, for most of the day, every day. I try to keep things pretty balanced and normal. I hope that's the right answer... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need, but I really don't think I've had anything quite like what you're describing. That's okay, right?\n\nDoctor: Have there ever been a few days in a row where you felt so energized, your mood was unusually high or irritable, and you were doing things much more quickly or intensely than usual — even if it was something positive like starting new projects, cleaning, or working faster — and this behavior was noticeable enough that others might have commented on it?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I... I don't believe so. I mean, I know I just talked about having those really good days where I'm getting a lot done around the house or at the warehouse, but... it's never been like a whole string of days where I'm acting out of the ordinary. \n\nI really do try to keep myself in check so I don't bother anyone. My husband might say I'm being a busybody sometimes, but he just means it affectionately, you know? He's never sat me down and told me I was acting strange or too intense for days on end. I just like to be helpful and keep things moving. I really hope I'm not giving you the wrong impression... I just don't think I've ever had a period like that where people were worried about me or anything. Is that what you were looking for? I just want to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: Have there ever been times when you felt so unusually excited, energetic, or on edge that it was clearly different from your usual self—even if it felt good or helped you get things done?\n\nPatient: Um, well, no, I really don't think so. I mean, I try very hard to just be steady, you know? I don't think I've ever felt so... out of sorts or overly excited that I wasn't still just being me. \n\nI know I can get a little enthusiastic when I'm sorting inventory at work or organizing the garage, but that's just because I want to do a good job. It's not like I'm a completely different person or acting strangely. I'm just being productive. I really try to make sure I'm always acting appropriate and not making a fuss. I hope that makes sense? I just don't think I've ever been so out of character that it would be, um, clearly different from my usual self. That's a good thing, right?\n\nDoctor: Have there ever been a few days in a row where you felt unusually energized or excited, even if it felt good, where you might have been more talkative, needed less sleep, or were doing things faster or more intensely than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess if I'm thinking about it, there are some days where I feel... maybe a little more upbeat than usual? Like, I'll wake up feeling really good, almost like I have too much energy, and I'll think, \"I can fix the whole house and reorganize the entire warehouse before dinner.\" And it feels wonderful, honestly, just being able to tackle everything. \n\nBut, um... sometimes, when I'm in that kind of mood, if my husband asks a question about one of my plans or tries to slow me down, I... I might snap at him a little bit. Just over something small, you know? I always feel terrible about it right after. It's not that I'm angry, I just get so focused and moving so fast. I try really hard not to be irritable with him. Is it normal to get a little snappy when you're just trying to get a lot done? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture... I'm really not an angry person, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when you felt so energized or excited that it was clearly different from your usual self—even if it felt good—where you might have been doing things faster, taking on more than usual, or felt like you didn’t need as much sleep?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I do have days where I just have so much energy, even after work. Usually, you know, moving freight and stocking all day, a person would be completely worn out by the time they clock out. But sometimes... I don't know, I just come home buzzing. \n\nInstead of sitting on the couch to rest, which I probably should do, sitting just feels... impossible. Like I have to keep moving. So I'll start reorganizing the kitchen cabinets, or I'll go out to the garage and sort all the storage bins. I just get so much accomplished! It's actually really helpful to get all those chores done, you know? I hope that doesn't sound like I'm doing something wrong. It's just nice to have the energy to keep the house looking nice after a long shift. That's a good thing, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Have there ever been times when your mood was so unusually high, you felt invincible, or you were unusually irritable along with feeling extremely energetic or active — even if it felt good — and this lasted for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I've ever felt... invincible. That sounds a bit extreme, doesn't it? I mean, I know I just said I can get really focused and maybe a tiny bit irritable when I'm trying to get things done, but it's never been like I thought I had superpowers or anything like that. \n\nI really just try to be a hard worker and take care of my home. I don't think my mood has ever been so high that it was a problem or lasted for days and days where I was completely out of control. I'm usually pretty grounded, I think. I hope I'm not giving you the impression that I'm, you know, unstable or anything. I just try to do my best every day. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have there ever been a few days in a row where you felt unusually upbeat or irritable, had a lot more energy than usual, and found yourself doing a lot more things—like taking on extra projects or cleaning a lot—more than you normally would, even if it felt good at the time?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess if you mean taking on extra things, then yes, sometimes I do that. Like at work, I've started volunteering for these extra safety-improvement projects. And outside of work, I've been signing up for a lot more art workshops than I usually would. \n\nI just get so motivated, you know? I'll sit down and make all these lists and schedules, almost like I'm running a whole operation or something. It feels really productive to be involved and to learn new things. I mean, it's good to be proactive at your job and have hobbies, right? I just want to make sure I'm doing things that are worthwhile. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm overdoing it... I just like to stay busy and be helpful. Is that the kind of thing you meant?\n\nDoctor: Can you recall a specific time when your mood was clearly different from usual—like feeling unusually high, elated, or irritable—while also feeling more energetic or active than normal, even if it felt good at the time?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose... sometimes I do get this feeling where I'm just so incredibly upbeat, almost too upbeat, if that makes sense? Like, I'll wake up and feel like I could completely fix up the whole house, and then go in and revamp everything at the warehouse, all before dinner. It's a wonderful feeling, honestly, being that capable. \n\nBut, um... I guess there is a downside, and I feel a little bad admitting this. When I get in that zone, if my husband even just gently questions one of my big plans, I... I can snap at him. Over something completely small and silly. I always feel terrible about it afterward because he's just trying to help, and I don't want to be a difficult wife. It's just that in the moment, I'm so focused and moving so fast. I hope you don't think I'm a mean person for getting short with him like that. It's just the stress of trying to get so much done, you know? Does that sound like what you're asking about?\n\nDoctor: Can you recall a specific time when you had a distinct period—lasting several days—where your mood was clearly different from usual, like feeling unusually high, elated, or irritable, along with feeling more energetic or active than normal, even if it felt good at the time?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know I just mentioned getting a little snappy with my husband, and having a lot of energy to do things around the house or at the warehouse, but... I don't really think I've had a time where it lasted for several days in a row and was, like, a completely different mood. \n\nI really try to keep things pretty steady, you know? I might have a really productive afternoon or a day where I'm just very motivated, but I don't think it's ever been a distinct period of days where I was completely unlike myself. I always try to make sure I'm doing a good job and being a good wife, and I feel like if I was acting really strange for days on end, I would notice, or my husband would say something more serious. I hope I'm answering this right... I just don't think it's ever been quite as extreme or long-lasting as what you're describing. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when you had a period that lasted at least four days in a row where your mood was clearly different from usual — like feeling unusually high, elated, or irritable — along with feeling much more energetic or active than normal, even if it felt good at the time?\n\nPatient: I know you're trying to get a clear picture, and I really want to give you the right answer, but... no, I honestly can't think of a time it lasted for four days straight. Four days is almost a whole work week, you know? \n\nIf I was acting that out of character, or... or snapping at my husband for that long, I'd feel just awful. And I'm sure someone at the warehouse would have pulled me aside if I was acting strange for that many shifts in a row. For me, it's really just a quick burst of energy to get through a busy day or organize things at home, and then I'm right back to my normal routine. \n\nI hope I'm not making your job harder by saying no... I just don't want to make something up if it didn't happen. Is it okay that I don't have an example like that?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
